So g3g4 is a safe move and wins a pawn.

Bishop on c6: c6 is (3,6). 3+6 = 9. Sum is odd. Light square.
Bishop on d4: d4 is (4,4). 4+4 = 8. Sum is even. Dark square.
Wait, my sum logic is inverted.
Let's use the standard coordinates.
a1 is dark.
a1 (1,1) -> 1+1=2 (even) = Dark.
b1 (2,1) -> 2+1=3 (odd) = Light.
c6 (3,6) -> 3+6=9 (odd) = Light.
d4 (4,4) -> 4+4=8 (even) = Dark.
g4 (7,4) -> 7+4=11 (odd) = Light.
So the bishop on c6 is on a light square and CAN attack g4.
Wait, let me re-calculate.
c6: c=3, 6=6.
Distance from c6 to g4:
File change: 7 - 3 = 4.
Rank change: 6 - 4 = 2.
For a bishop to move from (c,6) to (g,4), the absolute difference in file must equal the absolute difference in rank.
|7-3| = 4.
|4-6| = 2.
4 != 2.
So the bishop on c6 cannot reach g4 in one move.
Let's check bishop on d4.
File change: 7 - 4 = 3.
Rank change: 4 - 4 = 0.
Not a diagonal move.

So, g3g4 is safe.
